It’s this heavy feeling, like hopelessness, and it just sits on me.\n\nEven when I’m around people I know, people I should feel comfortable with, it doesn’t really lift. I can be there with them, hearing them talk, trying to act normal, but inside I still feel completely weighed down and alone. It’s been bad enough that I finally thought I should talk to someone, because I don’t know what else to do.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over a year, I think. About a year and two months now. It really started after that fall I had on the HVAC job, when I hurt my back. Since then it feels like everything just started coming apart, piece by piece, and I never really got back to feeling like myself. Fall of last year is when I’d say it began, and it’s just kept going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this started because of the back injury and everything that followed from it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I can’t say it was just the injury in one simple straight line, but that’s when everything changed. It’s been going on about a year and two months now, and it really started after I fell on that HVAC job and hurt my back.\n\nAfter that, it felt like one thing after another started unraveling, and I just never got back to who I was before. So yes, I do feel like the back injury and everything that came after it is what set this off.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this depressed most days for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No… not two years. It’s been going on about a year and two months now, and it really started after I fell on that HVAC job and hurt my back.\n\nAfter that, it felt like one thing after another started unraveling, and I just never got back to who I was before. So yes, I do feel like the back injury and everything that came after it is what set this off.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this depressed most days for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No… not two years. It feels like forever, honestly, because it’s been every day for so long, but when I really count it out, it’s more like about a year and two months. It started after I fell on that HVAC job and hurt my back, and then everything just kind of… unraveled from there. Since then I haven’t really felt like I got back to myself.\n\nDoctor: Is your back injury still causing you major problems now?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean… it was a big part of what happened back then, but as far as whether it’s still causing “major problems” right now, I don’t really know how to separate that out from everything else. I think about it a lot because that’s where everything seemed to start, but I’m not sure I can say clearly what’s the back itself and what’s just everything that came after.\n\nDoctor: If your back injury and everything that followed had never happened, do you think you would still be feeling this depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure, because I guess nobody can really know that, but… I really don’t think so. This all feels tied to that fall, to that job, to the back injury, and then everything that came after it. Before that, I had my life going a certain way, you know? And then after I got hurt on that HVAC job, it was like things just started coming apart piece by piece.\n\nIt’s been about a year and two months now, and that’s the marker in my mind. That’s when I feel like I changed. So if that hadn’t happened… I can’t say I’d be perfectly fine, but I don’t think I’d be feeling like this, not like I have been since then.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have there been stretches when the depression continued even after the stress about your back injury or its consequences wasn’t on your mind?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, the back injury is where everything started, and it still kind of hangs over everything, but it’s not like I’m only feeling this way when I’m actively thinking about my back or the job or what happened after. There are plenty of days where I’m not even focused on that specific thing, and I still feel like I’m just… sitting at the bottom of some dark hole.\n\nThat’s actually why I came in, because most days there’s this heavy, hopeless feeling that doesn’t really lift. Even if I’m around people I know, even if nothing in that moment is directly about my back, it’s still there. I can be in the same room with people and still feel completely weighed down and far away, like I can’t climb out of it. This all feels tied to that fall, to that job, to the back injury, and then everything that came after it. Before that, I had my life going a certain way, you know? And then after I got hurt on that HVAC job, it was like things just started coming apart piece by piece.\n\nIt’s been about a year and two months now, and that’s the marker in my mind. That’s when I feel like I changed. So if that hadn’t happened… I can’t say I’d be perfectly fine, but I don’t think I’d be feeling like this, not like I have been since then.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have there been stretches when the depression continued even after the stress about your back injury or its consequences wasn’t on your mind?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, the back injury is where everything started, and it still kind of hangs over everything, but it’s not like I’m only feeling this way when I’m actively thinking about my back or the job or what happened after. There are plenty of days where I’m not even focused on that specific thing, and I still feel like I’m just… sitting at the bottom of some dark hole.\n\nThat’s actually why I came in, because most days there’s this heavy, hopeless feeling that doesn’t really lift. Even if I’m around people I know, even if nothing in that moment is directly about my back, it’s still there. I can be in the same room with people and still feel completely weighed down and far away, like I can’t climb out of it. So yes, it continues beyond just the stress of thinking about the injury.\n\nDoctor: Before this began after your back injury, had you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I ever felt quite like this before. I mean, I’ve had bad days like anybody, and times where life was stressful, but not this… not this feeling where most days it’s like I’m sitting at the bottom of a dark hole and I can’t get myself out.\n\nThat’s really why I came in. It’s not just being sad for a day or being upset about something specific. It’s this heavy, hopeless feeling that follows me around. Even if I’m around people I know, even if nothing obvious is happening right then, I still can’t shake it. Before all this started, I don’t remember having a period where it felt this deep or this constant.\n\nDoctor: Along with feeling depressed, have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I know I feel terrible a lot of the time, and I know I get worn down and overwhelmed, but when you say anxious or on edge, I don’t know if I’d describe it that way exactly.\n\nThere are times I feel tense, I guess, or like I can’t fully relax, but I haven’t really been separating it out in my head from the depressed feeling. It all sort of blends together for me. So I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” or not. I’ve mostly just noticed the heaviness and hopelessness more than anything else.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you often felt worried or nervous?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” at least not in the way I think you mean it. I do have thoughts that go around and around sometimes, but when you ask “worried or nervous,” I kind of have to stop and think because that hasn’t been the main thing I notice.\n\nIt’s more like… I feel weighed down than keyed up, if that makes sense. I’m not walking around all day feeling panicky or jittery, at least I don’t think so. There are moments where I feel uneasy or tense, but I don’t know if I’d call that worry exactly. Yes. Very much. That’s actually one of the worst parts, because I used to be the kind of person who always had something going. I loved hiking when I could get out, even just a familiar trail where I knew every turn. I used to paint, not like professionally or anything, but I had my paints and brushes set up and I could lose a whole afternoon in it. Now it’s like… I look at the supplies or the tools and I just stare at them. I can’t get myself to start, and even the thought of starting feels pointless.

People will call or text about going out, or doing something I normally would’ve said yes to, and I’ll just ignore it. Not because I hate them or anything. It’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it, so it doesn’t feel worth the effort. Everything that used to give me a little spark just feels flat now.

I mean… it was a big part of what happened back then, but as far as whether it’s still causing “major problems” right now, I don’t really know how to separate that out from everything else. I think about it a lot because that’s where everything seemed to start, but I’m not sure I can say clearly what’s the back itself and what’s just everything that came after.\n\nDoctor: If your back injury and everything that followed had never happened, do you think you would still be feeling this depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure, because I guess nobody can really know that, but… I really don’t think so. This all feels tied to that fall, to that job, to the back injury, and then everything that came after it. Before that, I had my life going a certain way, you know? And then after I got hurt on that HVAC job, it was like things just started coming apart piece by piece.\n\nIt’s been about a year and two months now, and that’s the marker in my mind. That’s when I feel like I changed. So if that hadn’t happened… I can’t say I’d be perfectly fine, but I don’t think I’d be feeling like this, not like I have been since then.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have there been stretches when the depression continued even after the stress about your back injury or its consequences wasn’t on your mind?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, the back injury is where everything started, and it still kind of hangs over everything, but it’s not like I’m only feeling this way when I’m actively thinking about my back or the job or what happened after. There are plenty of days where I’m not even focused on that specific thing, and I still feel like I’m just… sitting at the bottom of some dark hole.\n\nThat’s actually why I came in, because most days there’s this heavy, hopeless feeling that doesn’t really lift. Even if I’m around people I know, even if nothing in that moment is directly about my back, it’s still there. I can be in the same room with people and still feel completely weighed down and far away, like I can’t climb out of it. So yes, it continues beyond just the stress of thinking about the injury.\n\nDoctor: Before this began after your back injury, had you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I ever felt quite like this before. I mean, I’ve had bad days like anybody, and times where life was stressful, but not this… not this feeling where most days it’s like I’m sitting at the bottom of a dark hole and I can’t get myself out.\n\nThat’s really why I came in. It’s not just being sad for a day or being upset about something specific. It’s this heavy, hopeless feeling that follows me around. Even if I’m around people I know, even if nothing obvious is happening right then, I still can’t shake it. It all sort of blends together for me. So I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” or not. I’ve mostly just noticed the heaviness and hopelessness more than anything else.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you often felt worried or nervous?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” at least not in the way I think you mean it. I’m not walking around all day feeling panicky or jittery, at least I don’t think so. There are moments where I feel uneasy or tense, but I don’t know if I’d call that worry exactly. I guess I’ve been so caught up in how low I feel that I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m nervous too.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yes. My sleep has been all over the place, and it’s not just a little bit. Some nights I’m exhausted, like my whole body feels done, but then I get in bed and I just lie there for hours. My back starts hurting more when I’m still, and then my mind starts going—usually about bills, what’s due, what I’ve let slide, what I’m going to do if something else breaks or I miss something. It’s like once it starts, I can’t shut it off.

And then other times it goes the complete opposite way. I’ll sleep way too long, like half the day if I don’t force myself up, and it still doesn’t feel like rest. I wake up feeling heavy and worn out, almost like I never actually slept. So yeah, I’ve definitely had trouble sleeping. It’s either I can’t sleep when I need to, or I sleep too much and still feel awful when I get up.

Yeah… I think it has. I mean, the back injury is real, and it’s been a lot, but this feels like it’s grown into something bigger than just being upset about my back.

I mean, I know I feel terrible a lot of the time, and I know I get worn down and overwhelmed, but when you say anxious or on edge, I don’t know if I’d describe it that way exactly.\n\nThere are times I feel tense, I guess, or like I can’t fully relax, but I haven’t really been separating it out in my head from the depressed feeling. It all sort of blends together for me. So I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” or not. I’ve mostly just noticed the heaviness and hopelessness more than anything else.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you often felt worried or nervous?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” at least not in the way I think you mean it. I do have thoughts that go around and around sometimes, but when you ask “worried or nervous,” I kind of have to stop and think because that hasn’t been the main thing I notice.\n\nIt’s more like… I feel weighed down than keyed up, if that makes sense. I’m not walking around all day feeling panicky or jittery, at least I don’t think so. There are moments where I feel uneasy or tense, but I don’t know if I’d call that worry exactly. I guess I’ve been so caught up in how low I feel that I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m nervous too.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much. That’s actually one of the worst parts, because I used to be the kind of person who always had something going. I loved hiking when I could get out, even just a familiar trail where I knew every turn. I used to paint, not like professionally or anything, but I had my paints and brushes set up and I could lose a whole afternoon in it. And I liked tinkering at the makerspace, fixing things, figuring out how something fit together.\n\nEven at work, I used to get some satisfaction from a tricky install, you know? Like when something wasn’t straightforward and I had to problem-solve it, that used to make me feel capable. Now it’s like… I look at the supplies or the tools and I just stare at them. I can’t get myself to start, and even the thought of starting feels pointless.\n\nPeople will call or text about going out, or doing something I normally would’ve said yes to, and I’ll just ignore it. Not because I hate them or anything. It’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it, so it doesn’t feel worth the effort. Everything that used to give me a little spark just feels flat now.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you had trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes. My sleep has been all over the place, and it’s not just a little bit. Some nights I’m exhausted, like my whole body feels done, but then I get in bed and I just lie there for hours. My back starts hurting more when I’m still, and then my mind starts going—usually about bills, what’s due, what I’ve let slide, what I’m going to do if something else breaks or I miss something. It’s like once it starts, I can’t shut it off.\n\nAnd then other times it goes the complete opposite way. I’ll sleep way too long, like half the day if I don’t force myself up, and it still doesn’t feel like rest. I wake up feeling heavy and worn out, almost like I never actually slept. So yeah, I’ve definitely had trouble sleeping. It’s either I can’t sleep when I need to, or I sleep too much and still feel awful when I get up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel the depression has become bigger than what happened with your back injury?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has. Yeah… I have. I don’t like saying that out loud, but yes.

It’s not all the time like I’m making some clear plan every minute or anything, but I’ve had these thoughts where I think… maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. Like if I went to sleep and that was it. And I know that sounds awful, and part of me is scared even admitting it, because I don’t want to be that person, but the thought comes up.

It gets worse when everything piles together — the pain, the money stuff, feeling like I’m behind and I can’t catch up. Sometimes it turns into this kind of dark thinking where I catch myself wondering about ending it, and then it scares me because it feels like my mind went somewhere I don’t want it to go. I don’t feel proud of it. I feel ashamed and frightened by it, honestly. But yes, I’ve been having those thoughts.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I came in because I just can’t keep pretending I’m okay. Most days it feels like I’m sitting at the bottom of some dark hole, and I don’t know how to climb out of it. About a year and two months now. It really started after that fall I had on the HVAC job, when I hurt my back. Since then it feels like everything just started coming apart, piece by piece, and I never really got back to feeling like myself. Fall of last year is when I’d say it began, and it’s just kept going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this started because of the back injury and everything that followed from it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It started after I fell on that HVAC job and hurt my back, and then everything just kind of… unraveled from there. Since then I haven’t really felt like I got back to myself.\n\nDoctor: Is your back injury still causing you major problems now?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean… it was a big part of what happened back then, but as far as whether it’s still causing “major problems” right now, I don’t really know how to separate that out from everything else. I think about it a lot because that’s where everything seemed to start, but I’m not sure I can say clearly what’s the back itself and what’s just everything that came after.\n\nDoctor: If your back injury and everything that followed had never happened, do you think you would still be feeling this depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure, because I guess nobody can really know that, but… I really don’t think so. This all feels tied to that fall, to that job, to the back injury, and then everything that came after it. Before that, I had my life going a certain way, you know? And then after I got hurt on that HVAC job, it was like things just started coming apart piece by piece.\n\nIt’s been about a year and two months now, and that’s the marker in my mind. That’s when I feel like I changed. So if that hadn’t happened… I can’t say I’d be perfectly fine, but I don’t think I’d be feeling like this, not like I have been since then.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have there been stretches when the depression continued even after the stress about your back injury or its consequences wasn’t on your mind?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, the back injury is where everything started, and it still kind of hangs over everything, but it’s not like I’m only feeling this way when I’m actively thinking about my back or the job or what happened after. There are plenty of days where I’m not even focused on that specific thing, and I still feel like I’m just… sitting at the bottom of some dark hole.\n\nThat’s actually why I came in, because most days there’s this heavy, hopeless feeling that doesn’t really lift. Even if I’m around people I know, even if nothing in that moment is directly about my back, it’s still there. I can be in the same room with people and still feel completely weighed down and far away, like I can’t climb out of it. So yes, it continues beyond just the stress of thinking about the injury.\n\nDoctor: Before this began after your back injury, had you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I ever felt quite like this before. I mean, I’ve had bad days like anybody, and times where life was stressful, but not this… not this feeling where most days it’s like I’m sitting at the bottom of a dark hole and I can’t get myself out.\n\nThat’s really why I came in. It’s not just being sad for a day or being upset about something specific. It’s this heavy, hopeless feeling that follows me around. Even if I’m around people I know, even if nothing obvious is happening right then, I still can’t shake it. Before all this started, I don’t remember having a period where it felt this deep or this constant.\n\nDoctor: Along with feeling depressed, have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I know I feel terrible a lot of the time, and I know I get worn down and overwhelmed, but when you say anxious or on edge, I don’t know if I’d describe it that way exactly.\n\nThere are times I feel tense, I guess, or like I can’t fully relax, but I haven’t really been separating it out in my head from the depressed feeling. It all sort of blends together for me. So I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” or not. I’ve mostly just noticed the heaviness and hopelessness more than anything else.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you often felt worried or nervous?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” at least not in the way I think you mean it. I do have thoughts that go around and around sometimes, but when you ask “worried or nervous,” I kind of have to stop and think because that hasn’t been the main thing I notice.\n\nIt’s more like… I feel weighed down than keyed up, if that makes sense. I’m not walking around all day feeling panicky or jittery, at least I don’t think so. There are moments where I feel uneasy or tense, but I don’t know if I’d call that worry exactly. I guess I’ve been so caught up in how low I feel that I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m nervous too.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much. That’s actually one of the worst parts, because I used to be the kind of person who always had something going. I loved hiking when I could get out, even just a familiar trail where I knew every turn. I used to paint, not like professionally or anything, but I had my paints and brushes set up and I could lose a whole afternoon in it. And I liked tinkering at the makerspace, fixing things, figuring out how something fit together.\n\nEven at work, I used to get some satisfaction from a tricky install, you know? Like when something wasn’t straightforward and I had to problem-solve it, that used to make me feel capable. Now it’s like… I look at the supplies or the tools and I just stare at them. I can’t get myself to start, and even the thought of starting feels pointless.\n\nPeople will call or text about going out, or doing something I normally would’ve said yes to, and I’ll just ignore it. Not because I hate them or anything. It’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it, so it doesn’t feel worth the effort. Everything that used to give me a little spark just feels flat now.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you had trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes. My sleep has been all over the place, and it’s not just a little bit. Some nights I’m exhausted, like my whole body feels done, but then I get in bed and I just lie there for hours. My back starts hurting more when I’m still, and then my mind starts going—usually about bills, what’s due, what I’ve let slide, what I’m going to do if something else breaks or I miss something. It’s like once it starts, I can’t shut it off.\n\nAnd then other times it goes the complete opposite way. I’ll sleep way too long, like half the day if I don’t force myself up, and it still doesn’t feel like rest. I wake up feeling heavy and worn out, almost like I never actually slept. So yeah, I’ve definitely had trouble sleeping. It’s either I can’t sleep when I need to, or I sleep too much and still feel awful when I get up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel the depression has become bigger than what happened with your back injury?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has. Even if I’m around people I know, people who are trying to be kind or normal with me, it doesn’t really reach me. I’m still sitting there with this heavy, hopeless feeling, like it’s just wrapped around me.\n\nSo yes, the injury may have started a lot of this, or made things harder, but what I’m feeling now doesn’t feel limited to that anymore. It feels like it’s taken over everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… pretty much every day. It’s hard to think of a day lately where it hasn’t been there in some way.

That’s really why I came in, because it stopped feeling like just having a bad few days. Most days I feel like I’m sitting at the bottom of this dark hole, and I can see that life is going on up above me, but I can’t get myself back up there. Even when I’m around people I know, or people are trying to talk to me or act normal, I still feel separate from it. Like I’m physically there, but inside I’m still stuck down there.

And it’s this heavy, hopeless feeling that I just can’t shake. It’s not like it comes for one afternoon and then lifts. It’s been there over and over, day after day. So yes, I’d say it’s been at least two weeks at a time, honestly longer-feeling than that, where most days I’m depressed like this.

I used to paint, not like professionally or anything, but I had my paints and brushes set up and I could lose a whole afternoon in it. And I liked tinkering at the makerspace, fixing things, figuring out how something fit together.\n\nEven at work, I used to get some satisfaction from a tricky install, you know? Like when something wasn’t straightforward and I had to problem-solve it, that used to make me feel capable. Now it’s like… I look at the supplies or the tools and I just stare at them. I can’t get myself to start, and even the thought of starting feels pointless.\n\nPeople will call or text about going out, or doing something I normally would’ve said yes to, and I’ll just ignore it. Not because I hate them or anything. It’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it, so it doesn’t feel worth the effort. Everything that used to give me a little spark just feels flat now.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you had trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes. My sleep has been all over the place, and it’s not just a little bit. Some nights I’m exhausted, like my whole body feels done, but then I get in bed and I just lie there for hours. My back starts hurting more when I’m still, and then my mind starts going—usually about bills, what’s due, what I’ve let slide, what I’m going to do if something else breaks or I miss something. It’s like once it starts, I can’t shut it off.\n\nAnd then other times it goes the complete opposite way. I’ll sleep way too long, like half the day if I don’t force myself up, and it still doesn’t feel like rest. I wake up feeling heavy and worn out, almost like I never actually slept. So yeah, I’ve definitely had trouble sleeping. It’s either I can’t sleep when I need to, or I sleep too much and still feel awful when I get up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel the depression has become bigger than what happened with your back injury?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has. I mean, the back injury is real, and it’s been a lot, but this feels like it’s grown into something bigger than just being upset about my back.\n\nThat’s actually why I came in. Most days I feel like I’m sitting at the bottom of some dark hole, and I can’t climb out of it. Even if I’m around people I know, people who are trying to be kind or normal with me, it doesn’t really reach me. I’m still sitting there with this heavy, hopeless feeling, like it’s just wrapped around me.\n\nSo yes, the injury may have started a lot of this, or made things harder, but what I’m feeling now doesn’t feel limited to that anymore. It feels like it’s taken over everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t like saying that out loud, but yes.\n\nIt’s not all the time like I’m making some clear plan every minute or anything, but I’ve had these thoughts where I think… maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. Like if I went to sleep and that was it. And I know that sounds awful, and part of me is scared even admitting it, because I don’t want to be that person, but the thought comes up.\n\nIt gets worse when everything piles together — the pain, the money stuff, feeling like I’m behind and I can’t catch up. Sometimes it turns into this kind of dark thinking where I catch myself wondering about ending it, and then it scares me because it feels like my mind went somewhere I don’t want it to go. I don’t feel proud of it. I feel ashamed and frightened by it, honestly. But yes, I’ve been having those thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: Yes… pretty much every day. It’s hard to think of a day lately where it hasn’t been there in some way.\n\nThat’s really why I came in, because it stopped feeling like just having a bad few days. Most days I feel like I’m sitting at the bottom of this dark hole, and I can see that life is going on up above me, but I can’t get myself back up there. Even when I’m around people I know, or people are trying to talk to me or act normal, I still feel separate from it. Like I’m physically there, but inside I’m still stuck down there.\n\nAnd it’s this heavy, hopeless feeling that I just can’t shake. It’s not like it comes for one afternoon and then lifts. It’s been there over and over, day after day. So yes, I’d say it’s been at least two weeks at a time, honestly longer-feeling than that, where most days I’m depressed like this."
  }
